============================================================
  PHASE 2: FrogNet HOT - serial requests, SAME/DIFF active
  Templates are cached on both ends. The daemon sends only the
  changed fields over the wire; the proxy reconstructs the full
  response locally. Serial performance is similar to Phase 1 -
  one request in flight at a time, so wire savings don't increase
  throughput here. The gain shows up in Phase 3 (concurrent).
============================================================

    Bytes sent:                 82,911   (      81.0 KB)
    Bytes without engine:      801,874   (     783.1 KB)
    Saved:                     718,963   ( 89.7%, 9.7x)

============================================================
  PHASE 3: RAMP - concurrent scalability (FrogNet HOT)
============================================================

  RAMP (FrogNet HOT) - 10 requests each at increasing concurrency
  What this shows: how throughput scales as more requests fly in parallel.
  With semantic compression, concurrent requests coalesce - the daemon
  executes once and fans out the result to all waiting callers.
  Higher concurrency = better wire efficiency. Watch req/s climb.

    conc=  1  ok=  1  p50=    69ms  elapsed=0.1s  rps=14.4
    conc=  5  ok=  5  p50=   120ms  elapsed=0.1s  rps=40.4
    conc= 10  ok= 10  p50=    67ms  elapsed=0.1s  rps=134.5
    conc= 25  ok= 25  p50=    74ms  elapsed=0.1s  rps=249.8
    conc= 50  ok= 50  p50=   126ms  elapsed=0.2s  rps=233.8

  RAMP wire:
    Bytes sent:                 23,177   (      22.6 KB)
    Bytes without engine:      224,525   (     219.3 KB)
    Saved:                     201,348   ( 89.7%, 9.7x)

============================================================
  PHASE 4: BURST - 30000 simultaneous requests (FrogNet HOT)
  This is where FrogNet shines. Concurrent requests coalesce on
  the daemon - it executes once and serves all callers. The wire
  carries tiny diff frames regardless of how many clients are waiting.
